Secondary: `device_name` is unindexed and unbounded, so multi-MB names persist at 30/min/IP.\n\n---\n\n### T5-10 — A non-string `license_key` produces a 500 instead of a 400\n**confidence: medium**\n\n**trace**\n1. `validate/route.ts:89` `if (!license_key)` — presence only; `123`, `[\"k\"]`, `{}` all pass\n2. `validate/route.ts:98` → `license-validation.ts:126` `licenseKey.trim()` → `TypeError: licenseKey.trim is not a function`\n3. `validate/route.ts:127-133` catch → **500** `{valid:false, error:\"Failed to validate license…\"}`\n\n**seam**: `route.ts:81-94` (the `as {license_key?: string}` cast is a compile-time-only guarantee) ↔ `license-validation.ts:126` (assumes a runtime string). Identical in `activate/route.ts:51`.\n\n**trigger_sketch**: `POST /api/license/validate {\"license_key\": 12345}`.\n\n**consequence**: malformed client input is reported as a server fault — it pollutes 5xx alerting, and via **T5-3** a 500 from the usage path is cached as `isValid:false` for an hour.\n\n---\n\n### T5-11 — The deactivate stub reports success for keys that do not exist, and is the only unrate-limited member of the cluster\n**confidence: medium (contract), low (exploitability)**\n\n**trace**: `deactivate/route.ts:17-43` — no `licenseValidateRateLimiter`, no `checkLicenseKey`, no DB touch; returns `{success:true, message:\"License deactivated successfully\"}` for **any** non-empty `license_key`. Aliased unauthenticated at `app/api/account/deactivate/route.ts:9`.\n\n**seam**: `deactivate/route.ts:39-42` ↔ every other member of the cluster, which all perform a real check. A client can reasonably conclude from a 200 that (a) the key was real and (b) server-side device state was released; neither holds.\n\n**trigger_sketch**: `POST /api/account/deactivate {\"license_key\":\"anything-at-all\"}` → 200 success.\n\n**consequence**: (1) the `device_validations` row survives deactivation, so a device the user believes they released still counts toward fair usage until its `lastValidatedAt` ages out of the `sinceDays` filter (`db-layer.ts:831-836`); (2) there is no server-side revocation for a leaked key — deactivate is UI theatre; (3) it is the one endpoint in the cluster with no rate limit, so it is a free unauthenticated `req.json()` body sink.\n\n---\n\n### T5-12 — `getClientIPFromHeaders` trust order + the `\"unknown\"` fallback share one bucket\n**confidence: low/medium**\n\n**trace**: `download-ip.ts:14-26` → `rate-limit.ts` key `ratelimit:license-validate:<ip>` (`index.js:992` `getKey = (identifier) => [this.prefix, identifier].join(\":\")`).\n\nTwo observations:\n- **Namespace is correctly shared** across `/api/license/validate`, `/api/license/activate`, and both `/api/account/*` aliases — the aliases are literal `export { POST }` re-exports (`app/api/account/validate/route.ts:12`), so they reuse the same module-level limiter instance and prefix. **An attacker cannot double their budget by alternating URLs.** Worth stating explicitly since it was the hypothesis.\n- **The fallback is the real seam**: if none of the four headers yields a plain IP, every such caller collapses onto the literal key `…:unknown` and shares one 30/min budget. On Vercel `x-vercel-forwarded-for` is platform-set, so this is safe there — but the trust order is `x-vercel-forwarded-for` → `cf-connecting-ip` → `x-forwarded-for` → `x-real-ip` with **no proxy-depth or provenance check**, so in any deployment where the Vercel header is absent (self-host, preview proxy, or Cloudflare-in-front-of-Vercel where the Vercel header holds the *CF edge* IP), `cf-connecting-ip` is fully attacker-chosen: `-H \"cf-connecting-ip: 1.2.3.<n>\"` rotates the bucket at will and restores unbounded Polar amplification.\n\n**consequence**: environment-dependent — either mass shared-bucket 429s (chaining into T5-1) or a trivially bypassed limiter.\n\n---\n\n### T5-13 — `getCreditBalance` performs a write transaction on the unauthenticated read path\n**confidence: low**\n\n**trace**: `validate/route.ts:115-116` `if (include_credits) getCreditBalance(license.userId)` → `db-layer.ts:641-653`: on any cache/grant divergence it opens `db.transaction(reconcileCreditBalance)`.\n\n**seam**: `route.ts:115` (treats `include_credits` as a cheap read flag; the value is unvalidated truthy) ↔ `db-layer.ts:652` (a write). Every brand-new user hits the drift branch at least once (`cachedRow` undefined → `cached = null`, `null !== 0`) — so the *first* credits-bearing validate for every account writes a `credit_balances` row. It converges (both `getCreditBalance` and `reconcileCreditBalance` call the same `getActiveGrantsTotal`, so the filters cannot mismatch — I checked for that specifically), so this is load, not corruption.\n\n**consequence**: an unauthenticated caller with any valid key can drive 30 write transactions/min/IP through a route documented as a validation read.\n\n---\n\n## depth_limits\n\n- **Not executed**: no requests were made against Polar, Upstash, or the DB — Polar's case/whitespace normalisation of license keys is **unverified**, so the \"canonical-casing key permanently misses `findAccountByKey` and hits Polar on every validate\" variant is stated only as the mechanism behind T5-6, not as a standalone finding.\n- **T5-6's index-check order** (`idx_account_keys_key` evaluated before `idx_account_keys_polar_license_key_id`) is inferred from index OID/creation order across migrations `0000` and `0007`; Postgres does not contractually guarantee it.
